Well, it gets a bit jumbled sometimes. When I try to explain things to people, my thoughts just sort of wander off track. I might start with one thought, like talking about the mail or something that happened at church, and before I know it, I end up talking about quilt patterns, or the birds outside, or... or warnings I feel like I'm getting.

Even on my better days, I feel like some of the people at my church and in my walking club are watching me. It seems like they're putting little messages about me in the church bulletin, or... or hiding things about me in what they say to each other. It's just very unsettling, and I felt like I needed to talk to someone about it.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been having these feelings of being watched?\n\nPatient: Well, let me think... it's been about eleven months now. That's how long I've been noticing the messages, and... well, hearing the voices, too. And just having trouble keeping my thoughts straight in general. It's all been happening for about eleven months.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about the voices you're hearing?\n\nPatient: Well, it usually happens when I'm at home and the house is quiet. I'll hear my name, or sometimes just a few words. It sounds exactly like my friend's voice coming from the kitchen, or out on the porch. It's so clear that I actually answer back out loud... before I catch myself and remember that nobody else is there.\n\nDoctor: What do the voices say when you hear them, are they telling you to do things or just talking about you?\n\nPatient: No, they don't tell me to do anything. And they aren't really having conversations about me, either. Like I said, it's mostly just my name... or maybe a quick 'hello' or 'are you there?' It's just a few words at a time, never a full sentence or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that the people at your church and in your walking club are actually hiding messages about you in what they say and the church bulletin?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. Honestly, that's why I finally came in to see someone. Even on my better days, when I'm feeling a bit more like myself, I just can't shake the feeling that they're watching me. It's like they're putting little messages about me in the church bulletin, or hiding things in the way they talk to each other when I'm around. It just feels very real to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the beliefs about people watching you and hiding messages are definitely true, or do you have some doubts about them?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess a part of me knows it sounds a little strange when I say it out loud... but yes, I really do believe it's true. Like I said, that's why I'm here. Even when my mood is a bit better and I'm not feeling quite so down, I just can't get rid of that feeling. I still feel like the people at church and in my walking club are keeping an eye on me, and that those little messages in the bulletin or in their conversations are definitely about me. It just feels too real to ignore.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe how your thoughts feel when you're trying to think clearly about something?\n\nPatient: Well, it gets a bit jumbled sometimes. When I try to explain things to people, my thoughts just sort of wander off track. I might start with one thought, like talking about the mail or something that happened at church, and before I know it, I end up talking about quilt patterns, or the birds outside, or... or warnings I feel like I'm getting. \n\nI don't always realize I'm doing it right away, but then I look at the person I'm talking to, and I can just see on their face that they don't know what I'm getting at. It's like I can't keep a straight line from the beginning of my thought to the end.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your beliefs about being watched and the hidden messages are all connected and part of a larger plan?\n\nPatient: I don't know if I'd call it a larger plan... I'm not really sure about that. I don't really know why they're doing it, or if it's part of something bigger, but I know it's happening.\n\nDoctor: When you're talking to someone and you start to wander off track, do you find it hard to get back to what you were originally saying?\n\nPatient: Yes, I really do. Like I was just saying, once I start off talking about the mail or something at church, and then I drift into talking about quilt patterns or the birds outside, or those warnings... I completely lose the thread. \n\nBy the time I see that look on their face and realize they don't know what I'm getting at, the first thought is just gone. I'm not really sure about that. I just know what I've been experiencing right in front of me. \n\nLike I was saying earlier, the whole reason I came in to see you is because even on my better days, I just can't shake the feeling that some of the people at church and in the walking club are watching me. I know they're putting little messages about me in the church bulletin, or hiding them in what they say when I'm around. I don't really know why they're doing it, or if it's part of something bigger, but I know it's happening.\n\nDoctor: When you're talking to someone and you start to wander off track, do you find it hard to get back to what you were originally saying?\n\nPatient: Yes, I really do. I completely lose the thread. \n\nBy the time I see that look on their face and realize they don't know what I'm getting at, the first thought is just gone. I'll try to stop and retrace my steps, but... no, I usually can't remember how to get back to what I originally meant to say. It just stays jumbled.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you felt extremely energetic or irritable, and your thoughts were racing?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really had anything like that.     "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n13\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF20.0, F23\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hello. Well, I came in because... I just can't shake this feeling I've been having. Even on my better days, I feel like some of the people at my church and in my walking club are watching me. It seems like they're putting little messages about me in the church bulletin, or... or hiding things about me in what they say to each other. It's just very unsettling, and I felt like I needed to talk to someone about it.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been having these feelings of being watched?\n\nPatient: Well, let me think... it's been about eleven months now. That's how long I've been noticing the messages, and... well, hearing the voices, too. And just having trouble keeping my thoughts straight in general. It's all been happening for about eleven months.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about the voices you're hearing?\n\nPatient: Well, it usually happens when I'm at home and the house is quiet. I'll hear my name, or sometimes just a few words. It sounds exactly like my friend's voice coming from the kitchen, or out on the porch. It's so clear that I actually answer back out loud... before I catch myself and remember that nobody else is there.\n\nDoctor: What do the voices say when you hear them, are they telling you to do things or just talking about you?\n\nPatient: No, they don't tell me to do anything. And they aren't really having conversations about me, either. Like I said, it's mostly just my name... or maybe a quick 'hello' or 'are you there?' It's just a few words at a time, never a full sentence or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that the people at your church and in your walking club are actually hiding messages about you in what they say and the church bulletin?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. Honestly, that's why I finally came in to see someone. Even on my better days, when I'm feeling a bit more like myself, I just can't shake the feeling that they're watching me. It's like they're putting little messages about me in the church bulletin, or hiding things in the way they talk to each other when I'm around. It just feels very real to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the beliefs about people watching you and hiding messages are definitely true, or do you have some doubts about them?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess a part of me knows it sounds a little strange when I say it out loud... but yes, I really do believe it's true. Like I said, that's why I'm here. Even when my mood is a bit better and I'm not feeling quite so down, I just can't get rid of that feeling. I still feel like the people at church and in my walking club are keeping an eye on me, and that those little messages in the bulletin or in their conversations are definitely about me. It just feels too real to ignore.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe how your thoughts feel when you're trying to think clearly about something?\n\nPatient: Well, it gets a bit jumbled sometimes. When I try to explain things to people, my thoughts just sort of wander off track. I might start with one thought, like talking about the mail or something that happened at church, and before I know it, I end up talking about quilt patterns, or the birds outside, or... or warnings I feel like I'm getting. \n\nI don't always realize I'm doing it right away, but then I look at the person I'm talking to, and I can just see on their face that they don't know what I'm getting at. It's like I can't keep a straight line from the beginning of my thought to the end.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your beliefs about being watched and the hidden messages are all connected and part of a larger plan?\n\nPatient: I don't know if I'd call it a larger plan... I'm not really sure about that. I just know what I've been experiencing right in front of me. \n\nLike I was saying earlier, the whole reason I came in to see you is because even on my better days, I just can't shake the feeling that some of the people at church and in the walking club are watching me. I know they're putting little messages about me in the church bulletin, or hiding them in what they say when I'm around. I don't really know why they're doing it, or if it's part of something bigger, but I know it's happening.\n\nDoctor: When you're talking to someone and you start to wander off track, do you find it hard to get back to what you were originally saying?\n\nPatient: Yes, I really do. Like I was just saying, once I start off talking about the mail or something at church, and then I drift into talking about quilt patterns or the birds outside, or those warnings... I completely lose the thread. \n\nBy the time I see that look on their face and realize they don't know what I'm getting at, the first thought is just gone. I'll try to stop and retrace my steps, but... no, I usually can't remember how to get back to what I originally meant to say. It just stays jumbled.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you felt extremely energetic or irritable, and your thoughts were racing?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really had anything like that. I don't get bursts of energy or feel like my thoughts are racing ahead of me. If anything, I usually feel pretty tired and just... down, most of the time. I might get frustrated when I can't think straight, but I wouldn't say I get extremely irritable or wound up. It's really just the jumbled thoughts and feeling like people are watching me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble taking care of your daily activities, like bathing, dressing, or managing your household?\n\nPatient: Well, I still live at home, but... I guess things have slipped a bit. I haven't been keeping up with my quilting gatherings or the walking club lately. And I just haven't had the energy for much of my gardening, either. My daughter has actually started coming over more often to check on me... making sure I'm eating my meals and keeping track of my appointments. So, I suppose I am having a little more trouble than I used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you been doing things that don't make sense to you or to other people?\n\nPatient: Yes, I guess I have. There are mornings when I find myself just standing by the front door with my purse in my hand for a long time. I'm not going out, and... I don't really know what I was about to do. It just doesn't make sense to me when I catch myself doing that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think all these things that are happening to you, like the voices and the messages, are all connected in some way?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure if they're connected. I haven't really thought about it like that. I just know the voices happen, and then sometimes I feel like people are watching me or sending me messages, but... I don't know if there's a bigger reason behind all of it. It just feels like a lot of confusing things happening at once.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hello. Well, I came in because... I just can't shake this feeling I've been having. Even on my better days, I feel like some of the people at my church and in my walking club are watching me. It seems like they're putting little messages about me in the church bulletin, or... or hiding things about me in what they say to each other. It's just very unsettling, and I felt like I needed to talk to someone about it.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been having these feelings of being watched?\n\nPatient: Well, let me think... it's been about eleven months now. That's how long I've been noticing the messages, and... well, hearing the voices, too. And just having trouble keeping my thoughts straight in general. It's all been happening for about eleven months.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about the voices you're hearing?\n\nPatient: Well, it usually happens when I'm at home and the house is quiet. I'll hear my name, or sometimes just a few words. It sounds exactly like my friend's voice coming from the kitchen, or out on the porch. It's so clear that I actually answer back out loud... before I catch myself and remember that nobody else is there.\n\nDoctor: What do the voices say when you hear them, are they telling you to do things or just talking about you?\n\nPatient: No, they don't tell me to do anything. And they aren't really having conversations about me, either. Like I said, it's mostly just my name... or maybe a quick 'hello' or 'are you there?' It's just a few words at a time, never a full sentence or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that the people at your church and in your walking club are actually hiding messages about you in what they say and the church bulletin?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. Honestly, that's why I finally came in to see someone. Even on my better days, when I'm feeling a bit more like myself, I just can't shake the feeling that they're watching me. It's like they're putting little messages about me in the church bulletin, or hiding things in the way they talk to each other when I'm around. It just feels very real to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the beliefs about people watching you and hiding messages are definitely true, or do you have some doubts about them?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess a part of me knows it sounds a little strange when I say it out loud... but yes, I really do believe it's true. Like I said, that's why I'm here. Even when my mood is a bit better and I'm not feeling quite so down, I just can't get rid of that feeling. I still feel like the people at church and in my walking club are keeping an eye on me, and that those little messages in the bulletin or in their conversations are definitely about me. It just feels too real to ignore.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe how your thoughts feel when you're trying to think clearly about something?\n\nPatient: Well, it gets a bit jumbled sometimes. When I try to explain things to people, my thoughts just sort of wander off track. I might start with one thought, like talking about the mail or something that happened at church, and before I know it, I end up talking about quilt patterns, or the birds outside, or... or warnings I feel like I'm getting. \n\nI don't always realize I'm doing it right away, but then I look at the person I'm talking to, and I can just see on their face that they don't know what I'm getting at. It's like I can't keep a straight line from the beginning of my thought to the end.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your beliefs about being watched and the hidden messages are all connected and part of a larger plan?\n\nPatient: I don't know if I'd call it a larger plan... I'm not really sure about that. I just know what I've been experiencing right in front of me. \n\nLike I was saying earlier, the whole reason I came in to see you is because even on my better days, I just can't shake the feeling that some of the people at church and in the walking club are watching me. I know they're putting little messages about me in the church bulletin, or hiding them in what they say when I'm around. I don't really know why they're doing it, or if it's part of something bigger, but I know it's happening.\n\nDoctor: When you're talking to someone and you start to wander off track, do you find it hard to get back to what you were originally saying?\n\nPatient: Yes, I really do. It just stays jumbled.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you felt extremely energetic or irritable, and your thoughts were racing?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really had anything like that. My daughter has actually started coming over more often to check on me... making sure I'm eating my meals and keeping track of my appointments. It's like they're putting little messages about me in the church bulletin, or hiding things in the way they talk to each other when I'm around. It just feels very real to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the beliefs about people watching you and hiding messages are definitely true, or do you have some doubts about them?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess a part of me knows it sounds a little strange when I say it out loud... but yes, I really do believe it's true. Like I said, that's why I'm here. Even when my mood is a bit better and I'm not feeling quite so down, I just can't get rid of that feeling. S020\n  name: Hallucinations\n  description: Perception-like experiences that occur without an external stimulus and are as vivid and clear as normal perception.\n  manifestation: Sometimes when the house is quiet I hear my name or a few words in my friend's voice from the kitchen or porch, clear enough that I answer before I remember nobody is there.\n- key: S021\n  name: Disorganized_Thinking_Speech\n  description: Symptoms where speech or thought appears scattered and difficult to follow due to a lack of logical or consistent flow.\n  manifestation: When I try to explain things, I start with one thought about the mail or church and end up talking about quilt patterns, birds, and warnings, and then I can see people don't know what I'm getting at.\n- key: S022\n manifestation: There are mornings when I find myself standing by the front door with my purse in my hand for a long time, not going out and not really knowing what I was about to do.\n- key: S023\n  name: Negative_Symptoms\n  description: A state where emotions, motivation, speech, or social interest that should normally be present are diminished or lost.\n  manifestation: I don't talk much at coffee hour anymore, and even when someone tells a funny story I just sit there with a flat face like the feeling can't get out.\n- key: S025\n  name: Loss_of_Interest\n  description: A state where interest or pleasure in previously enjoyed activities is markedly diminished.\n  manifestation: I used to love my garden, quilting, and reading before bed, but now I mostly leave the quilt basket untouched and the weeds can grow without me caring much.\n- key: S026\n  name: Weight_Appetite_Change\n  description: Significant increase or decrease in appetite or weight.\n  manifestation: Most days food just doesn't appeal to me, so I pick at toast or soup and my skirts have been hanging looser than they used to.\n- key: S029\n  name: Fatigue\n  description: Persistent fatigue that is not relieved by rest and a marked lack of energy.\n  manifestation: Even after a full night in bed, carrying laundry or walking to the mailbox can make my body feel heavy, like I've already done a day's work.\n- key: S030\n  name: Worthlessness_Guilt\n  description: Feelings of worthlessness or excessive or inappropriate guilt (which may be delusional; not merely self-reproach or guilt about being sick).\n  manifestation: I keep going over every little thing I might have done wrong, especially not calling my friend more, and I feel like I must have failed her and everybody else.\n- key: S032\n  name: Death_Suicide\n  description: Recurrent thoughts of death, recurrent suicidal ideation, or a suicide attempt.\n  manifestation: I don't have a plan to hurt myself, but I catch myself thinking a major mood episode (Criterion B)\n  manifestation: There was a spell of several weeks when I was getting dressed, eating a bit better, and not feeling so weighed down, but I still heard my name in What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hello. Well, I came in because... I just can't shake this feeling I've been having. Even on my better days, I feel like some of the people at my church and in my walking club are watching me. It seems like they're putting little messages about me in the church bulletin, or... or hiding things about me in what they say to each other. It's just very unsettling, and I felt like I needed to talk to someone about it.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been having these feelings of being watched?\n\nPatient: Well, let me think... it's been about eleven months now. That's how long I've been noticing the messages, and... well, hearing the voices, too. And just having trouble keeping my thoughts straight in general. It's all been happening for about eleven months.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about the voices you're hearing?\n\nPatient: Well, it usually happens when I'm at home and the house is quiet. I'll hear my name, or sometimes just a few words. It sounds exactly like my friend's voice coming from the kitchen, or out on the porch. It's so clear that I actually answer back out loud... before I catch myself and remember that nobody else is there.\n\nDoctor: What do the voices say when you hear them, are they telling you to do things or just talking about you?\n\nPatient: No, they don't tell me to do anything. And they aren't really having conversations about me, either. Like I said, it's mostly just my name... or maybe a quick 'hello' or 'are you there?' It's just a few words at a time, never a full sentence or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that the people at your church and in your walking club are actually hiding messages about you in what they say and the church bulletin?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. Honestly, that's why I finally came in to see someone. Even on my better days, when I'm feeling a bit more like myself, I just can't shake the feeling that they're watching me. It's like they're putting little messages about me in the church bulletin, or hiding things in the way they talk to each other when I'm around. It just feels very real to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the beliefs about people watching you and hiding messages are definitely true, or do you have some doubts about them?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess a part of me knows it sounds a little strange when I say it out loud... but yes, I really do believe it's true. Like I said, that's why I'm here. Even when my mood is a bit better and I'm not feeling quite so down, I just can't get rid of that feeling. I still feel like the people at church and in my walking club are keeping an eye on me, and that those little messages in the bulletin or in their conversations are definitely about me. It just feels too real to ignore.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe how your thoughts feel when you're trying to think clearly about something?\n\nPatient: Well, it gets a bit jumbled sometimes. When I try to explain things to people, my thoughts just sort of wander off track. I might start with one thought, like talking about the mail or something that happened at church, and before I know it, I end up talking about quilt patterns, or the birds outside, or... or warnings I feel like I'm getting. \n\nI don't always realize I'm doing it right away, but then I look at the person I'm talking to, and I can just see on their face that they don't know what I'm getting at. It's like I can't keep a straight line from the beginning of my thought to the end.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your beliefs about being watched and the hidden messages are all connected and part of a larger plan?\n\nPatient: I don't know if I'd call it a larger plan... I'm not really sure about that. I just know what I've been experiencing right in front of me. \n\nLike I was saying earlier, the whole reason I came in to see you is because even on my better days, I just can't shake the feeling that some of the people at church and in the walking club are watching me. I know they're putting little messages about me in the church bulletin, or hiding them in what they say when I'm around. I don't really know why they're doing it, or if it's part of something bigger, but I know it's happening.\n\nDoctor: When you're talking to someone and you start to wander off track, do you find it hard to get back to what you were originally saying?\n\nPatient: Yes, I really do. Like I was just saying, once I start off talking about the mail or something at church, and then I drift into talking about quilt patterns or the birds outside, or those warnings... I completely lose the thread. \n\nBy the time I see that look on their face and realize they don't know what I'm getting at, the first thought is just gone. I'll try to stop and retrace my steps, but... no, I usually can't remember how to get back to what I originally meant to say. It just stays jumbled.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you felt extremely energetic or irritable, and your thoughts were racing?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really had anything like that. I don't get bursts of energy or feel like my thoughts are racing ahead of me. If anything, I usually feel pretty tired and just... down, most of the time. I might get frustrated when I can't think straight, but I wouldn't say I get extremely irritable or wound up. It's really just the jumbled thoughts and feeling like people are watching me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble taking care of your daily activities, like bathing, dressing, or managing your household?\n\nPatient: Well, I still live at home, but... I guess things have slipped a bit. I haven't been keeping up with my quilting gatherings or the walking club lately. And I just haven't had the energy for much of my gardening, either. My daughter has actually started coming over more often to check on me... making sure I'm eating my meals and keeping track of my appointments. So, I suppose I am having a little more trouble than I used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you been doing things that don't make sense to you or to other people?\n\nPatient: Yes, I guess I have. There are mornings when I find myself just standing by the front door with my purse in my hand for a long time. I'm not going out, and... I don't really know what I was about to do. It just doesn't make sense to me when I catch myself doing that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think all these things that are happening to you, like the voices and the messages, are all connected in some way?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure if they're connected. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hello. Well, I came in because... I just can't shake this feeling I've been having. Even on my better days, I feel like some of the people at my church and in my walking club are watching me. It seems like they're putting little messages about me in the church bulletin, or... or hiding things about me in what they say to each other. It's just very unsettling, and I felt like I needed to talk to someone about it.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been having these feelings of being watched?\n\nPatient: Well, let me think... it's been about eleven months now. That's how long I've been noticing the messages, and... well, hearing the voices, too. And just having trouble keeping my thoughts straight in general. It's all been happening for about eleven months.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about the voices you're hearing?\n\nPatient: Well, it usually happens when I'm at home and the house is quiet. I'll hear my name, or sometimes just a few words. It sounds exactly like my friend's voice coming from the kitchen, or out on the porch. It's so clear that I actually answer back out loud... before I catch myself and remember that nobody else is there.\n\nDoctor: What do the voices say when you hear them, are they telling you to do things or just talking about you?\n\nPatient: No, they don't tell me to do anything. And they aren't really having conversations about me, either. Like I said, it's mostly just my name... or maybe a quick 'hello' or 'are you there?' It's just a few words at a time, never a full sentence or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that the people at your church and in your walking club are actually hiding messages about you in what they say and the church bulletin?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. Honestly, that's why I finally came in to see someone. Even on my better days, when I'm feeling a bit more like myself, I just can't shake the feeling that they're watching me. It's like they're putting little messages about me in the church bulletin, or hiding things in the way they talk to each other when I'm around. It just feels very real to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the beliefs about people watching you and hiding messages are definitely true, or do you have some doubts about them?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess a part of me knows it sounds a little strange when I say it out loud... but yes, I really do believe it's true. Like I said, that's why I'm here. Even when my mood is a bit better and I'm not feeling quite so down, I just can't get rid of that feeling. I still feel like the people at church and in my walking club are keeping an eye on me, and that those little messages in the bulletin or in their conversations are definitely about me. It just feels too real to ignore.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe how your thoughts feel when you're trying to think clearly about something?\n\nPatient: Well, it gets a bit jumbled sometimes. When I try to explain things to people, my thoughts just sort of wander off track. I might start with one thought, like talking about the mail or something that happened at church, and before I know it, I end up talking about quilt patterns, or the birds outside, or... or warnings I feel like I'm getting. \n\nI don't always realize I'm doing it right away, but then I look at the person I'm talking to, and I can just see on their face that they don't know what I'm getting at. It's like I can't keep a straight line from the beginning of my thought to the end.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your beliefs about being watched and the hidden messages are all connected and part of a larger plan?\n\nPatient: I don't know if I'd call it a larger plan... I'm not really sure about that. I just know what I've been experiencing right in front of me. \n\nLike I was saying earlier, the whole reason I came in to see you is because even on my better days, I just can't shake the feeling that some of the people at church and in the walking club are watching me. I know they're putting little messages about me in the church bulletin, or hiding them in what they say when I'm around. I don't really know why they're doing it, or if it's part of something bigger, but I know it's happening.\n\nDoctor: When you're talking to someone and you start to wander off track, do you find it hard to get back to what you were originally saying?\n\nPatient: Yes, I really do. Like I was just saying, once I start off talking about the mail or something at church, and then I drift into talking about quilt patterns or the birds outside, or those warnings... I completely lose the thread. \n\nBy the time I see that look on their face and realize they don't know what I'm getting at, the first thought is just gone. I'll try to stop and retrace my steps, but... no, I usually can't remember how to get back to what I originally meant to say. It just stays jumbled.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you felt extremely energetic or irritable, and your thoughts were racing?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really had anything like that. I don't get bursts of energy or feel like my thoughts are racing ahead of me. If anything, I usually feel pretty tired and just... down, most of the time. I might get frustrated when I can't think straight, but I wouldn't say I get extremely irritable or wound up. It's really just the jumbled thoughts and feeling like people are watching me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble taking care of your daily activities, like bathing, dressing, or managing your household?\n\nPatient: Well, I still live at home, but... I guess things have slipped a bit. I haven't been keeping up with my quilting gatherings or the walking club lately. And I just haven't had the energy for much of my gardening, either. My daughter has actually started coming over more often to check on me... making sure I'm eating my meals and keeping track of my appointments. So, I suppose I am having a little more trouble than I used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you been doing things that don't make sense to you or to other people?\n\nPatient: Yes, I guess I have. There are mornings when I find myself just standing by the front door with my purse in my hand for a long time. I'm not going out, and... I don't really know what I was about to do. It just doesn't make sense to me when I catch myself doing that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think all these things that are happening to you, like the voices and the messages, are all connected in some way?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure if they're connected. I haven't really thought about it like that. I just know the voices happen, and then sometimes I feel like people are watching me or sending me messages, but... I don't know if there's a bigger reason behind all of it. It just feels like a lot of confusing things happening at once.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you felt extremely depressed or hopeless, separate from the feelings of being watched?\n\nPatient: Well, I wouldn't really say they were separate. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n18\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF20.0, F23\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hello. Well, I came in because... I just can't shake this feeling I've been having. Even on my better days, I feel like some of the people at my church and in my walking club are watching me. It seems like they're putting little messages about me in the church bulletin, or... or hiding things about me in what they say to each other. It's just very unsettling, and I felt like I needed to talk to someone about it.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been having these feelings of being watched?\n\nPatient: Well, let me think... it's been about eleven months now. That's how long I've been noticing the messages, and... well, hearing the voices, too. And just having trouble keeping my thoughts straight in general. It's all been happening for about eleven months.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about the voices you're hearing?\n\nPatient: Well, it usually happens when I'm at home and the house is quiet. I'll hear my name, or sometimes just a few words. It sounds exactly like my friend's voice coming from the kitchen, or out on the porch. It's so clear that I actually answer back out loud... before I catch myself and remember that nobody else is there.\n\nDoctor: What do the voices say when you hear them, are they telling you to do things or just talking about you?\n\nPatient: No, they don't tell me to do anything. And they aren't really having conversations about me, either. Like I said, it's mostly just my name... or maybe a quick 'hello' or 'are you there?' It's just a few words at a time, never a full sentence or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that the people at your church and in your walking club are actually hiding messages about you in what they say and the church bulletin?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. Honestly, that's why I finally came in to see someone. Even on my better days, when I'm feeling a bit more like myself, I just can't shake the feeling that they're watching me. It's like they're putting little messages about me in the church bulletin, or hiding things in the way they talk to each other when I'm around. It just feels very real to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the beliefs about people watching you and hiding messages are definitely true, or do you have some doubts about them?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess a part of me knows it sounds a little strange when I say it out loud... but yes, I really do believe it's true. Like I said, that's why I'm here. Even when my mood is a bit better and I'm not feeling quite so down, I just can't get rid of that feeling. I still feel like the people at church and in my walking club are keeping an eye on me, and that those little messages in the bulletin or in their conversations are definitely about me. It just feels too real to ignore.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe how your thoughts feel when you're trying to think clearly about something?\n\nPatient: Well, it gets a bit jumbled sometimes. When I try to explain things to people, my thoughts just sort of wander off track. I might start with one thought, like talking about the mail or something that happened at church, and before I know it, I end up talking about quilt patterns, or the birds outside, or... or warnings I feel like I'm getting. \n\nI don't always realize I'm doing it right away, but then I look at the person I'm talking to, and I can just see on their face that they don't know what I'm getting at. It's like I can't keep a straight line from the beginning of my thought to the end.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your beliefs about being watched and the hidden messages are all connected and part of a larger plan?\n\nPatient: I don't know if I'd call it a larger plan... I'm not really sure about that. I just know what I've been experiencing right in front of me. \n\nLike I was saying earlier, the whole reason I came in to see you is because even on my better days, I just can't shake the feeling that some of the people at church and in the walking club are watching me. I know they're putting little messages about me in the church bulletin, or hiding them in what they say when I'm around. I don't really know why they're doing it, or if it's part of something bigger, but I know it's happening.\n\nDoctor: When you're talking to someone and you start to wander off track, do you find it hard to get back to what you were originally saying?\n\nPatient: Yes, I really do. Like I was just saying, once I start off talking about the mail or something at church, and then I drift into talking about quilt patterns or the birds outside, or those warnings... I completely lose the thread. \n\nBy the time I see that look on their face and realize they don't know what I'm getting at, the first thought is just gone. I'll try to stop and retrace my steps, but... no, I usually can't remember how to get back to what I originally meant to say. It just stays jumbled.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you felt extremely energetic or irritable, and your thoughts were racing?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really had anything like that. I don't get bursts of energy or feel like my thoughts are racing ahead of me. If anything, I usually feel pretty tired and just... down, most of the time. I might get frustrated when I can't think straight, but I wouldn't say I get extremely irritable or wound up. It's really just the jumbled thoughts and feeling like people are watching me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble taking care of your daily activities, like bathing, dressing, or managing your household?\n\nPatient: Well, I still live at home, but... I guess things have slipped a bit. I haven't been keeping up with my quilting gatherings or the walking club lately. And I just haven't had the energy for much of my gardening, either. My daughter has actually started coming over more often to check on me... making sure I'm eating my meals and keeping track of my appointments. So, I suppose I am having a little more trouble than I used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you been doing things that don't make sense to you or to other people?\n\nPatient: Yes, I guess I have. There are mornings when I find myself just standing by the front door with my purse in my hand for a long time. I'm not going out, and... I don't really know what I was about to do. It just doesn't make sense to me when I catch myself doing that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think all these things that are happening to you, like the voices and the messages, are all connected in some way?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure if they're connected. I haven't really thought about it like that. I just know the voices happen, and then sometimes I feel like people are watching me or sending me messages, but... I don't know if there's a bigger reason behind all of it. It just feels like a lot of confusing things happening at once.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you felt extremely depressed or hopeless, separate from the feelings of being watched?\n\nPatient: Well, I wouldn't really say they were separate. Um... when I was at my absolute lowest—when I was barely eating and just sitting there thinking I had completely failed everyone in my life... that was actually the exact same time I was hearing my friend's voice. And... it was during that time I really believed the people from church were sending those messages about me. So, no, they kind of happened all together, rather than separately.\n\nDoctor: Have you been doing things that you later realize don't make sense, or that other people point out don't make sense?\n\nPatient: Well, like I mentioned a minute ago, it's really just that thing with my purse by the front door. I can't think of anything else besides that. And... no, nobody else has pointed anything out to me. It's mostly just me catching myself doing that one thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you been engaging in any behaviors that you or others consider bizarre or unusual?\n\nPatient: I think that's basically what we were just talking about... um, it's really just the purse thing I mentioned a second ago. I mean, I can still get things done around the house just fine. I haven't noticed any trouble following instructions or anything like that. So, no, I wouldn't say I'm disorganized in that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant changes in your mood or emotional state over the past eleven months?\n\nPatient: Well, yes, I have. Over this past year... um, when I was at my absolute lowest, I was just so down that I was barely eating. I just kept thinking I had completely failed everyone in my life. And... well, what was really hard was that while I was feeling that low, I was also hearing my friend's voice when she wasn't even there. And I really started believing that the people at my church were sending secret messages about me. So, yes, it got pretty bad for a while there.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. So, no, they kind of happened all together, rather than separately.\n\nDoctor: Have you been doing things that you later realize don't make sense, or that other people point out don't make sense?\n\nPatient: Well, like I mentioned a minute ago, it's really just that thing with my purse by the front door. I can't think of anything else besides that. And... no, nobody else has pointed anything out to me. It's mostly just me catching myself doing that one thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you been engaging in any behaviors that you or others consider bizarre or unusual?\n\nPatient: I think that's basically what we were just talking about... um, it's really just the purse thing I mentioned a second ago. Besides that, no. I mean, I can still get things done around the house just fine. I haven't noticed any trouble following instructions or anything like that. So, no, I wouldn't say I'm disorganized in that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant changes in your mood or emotional state over the past eleven months?\n\nPatient: Well, yes, I have. It seems like they're putting little messages about me in the church bulletin, or... or hiding things about me in what they say to each other. That's how long I've been noticing the messages, and... well, hearing the voices, too. And just having trouble keeping my thoughts straight in general. It's all been happening for about eleven months.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about the voices you're hearing?\n\nPatient: Well, it usually happens when I'm at home and the house is quiet. I'll hear my name, or sometimes just a few words. It sounds exactly like my friend's voice coming from the kitchen, or out on the porch. It's so clear that I actually answer back out loud... before I catch myself and remember that nobody else is there.\n\nDoctor: What do the voices say when you hear them, are they telling you to do things or just talking about you?\n\nPatient: No, they don't tell me to do anything. And they aren't really having conversations about me, either. Like I said, it's mostly just my name... or maybe a quick 'hello' or 'are you there?' It's just a few words at a time, never a full sentence or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that the people at your church and in your walking club are actually hiding messages about you in what they say and the church bulletin?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. Honestly, that's why I finally came in to see someone. Even on my better days, when I'm feeling a bit more like myself, I just can't shake the feeling that they're watching me. It's like they're putting little messages about me in the church bulletin, or hiding things in the way they talk to each other when I'm around. It just feels very real to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the beliefs about people watching you and hiding messages are definitely true, or do you have some doubts about them?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess a part of me knows it sounds a little strange when I say it out loud... but yes, I really do believe it's true. Like I said, that's why I'm here. Even when my mood is a bit better and I'm not feeling quite so down, I just can't get rid of that feeling. I still feel like the people at church and in my walking club are keeping an eye on me, and that those little messages in the bulletin or in their conversations are definitely about me. It just feels too real to ignore.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe how your thoughts feel when you're trying to think clearly about something?\n\nPatient: Well, it gets a bit jumbled sometimes. When I try to explain things to people, my thoughts just sort of wander off track. It's like I can't keep a straight line from the beginning of my thought to the end.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your beliefs about being watched and the hidden messages are all connected and part of a larger plan?\n\nPatient: I don't know if I'd call it a larger plan... I'm not really sure about that. I just know what I've been experiencing right in front of me. \n\nLike I was saying earlier, the whole reason I came in to see you is because even on my better days, I just can't shake the feeling that some of the people at church and in the walking club are watching me. I know they're putting little messages about me in the church bulletin, or hiding them in what they say when I'm around. I don't really know why they're doing it, or if it's part of something bigger, but I know it's happening.\n\nDoctor: When you're talking to someone and you start to wander off track, do you find it hard to get back to what you were originally saying?\n\nPatient: Yes, I really do. It's like I can't keep a straight line from the beginning of my thought to the end.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your beliefs about being watched and the hidden messages are all connected and part of a larger plan?\n\nPatient: I don't know if I'd call it a larger plan... I'm not really sure about that. I just know what I've been experiencing right in front of me. \n\nLike I was saying earlier, the whole reason I came in to see you is because even on my better days, I just can't shake the feeling that some of the people at church and in the walking club are watching me. I know they're putting little messages about me in the church bulletin, or hiding them in what they say when I'm around. I don't really know why they're doing it, or if it's part of something bigger, but I know it's happening.\n\nDoctor: When you're talking to someone and you start to wander off track, do you find it hard to get back to what you were originally saying?\n\nPatient: Yes, I really do. Like I was just saying, once I start off talking about the mail or something at church, and then I drift into talking about quilt patterns or the birds outside, or those warnings... I completely lose the thread. \n\nBy the time I see that look on their face and realize they don't know what I'm getting at, the first thought is just gone. I'll try to stop and retrace my steps, but... no, I usually can't remember how to get back to what I originally meant to say. It just stays jumbled.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you felt extremely energetic or irritable, and your thoughts were racing?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really had anything like that. I don't get bursts of energy or feel like my thoughts are racing ahead of me. If anything, I usually feel pretty tired and just... down, most of the time. I might get frustrated when I can't think straight, but I wouldn't say I get extremely irritable or wound up. It's really just the jumbled thoughts and feeling like people are watching me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble taking care of your daily activities, like bathing, dressing, or managing your household?\n\nPatient: Well, I still live at home, but... I guess things have slipped a bit. I haven't been keeping up with my quilting gatherings or the walking club lately. And I just haven't had the energy for much of my gardening, either. My daughter has actually started coming over more often to check on me... making sure I'm eating my meals and keeping track of my appointments. So, I suppose I am having a little more trouble than I used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you been doing things that don't make sense to you or to other people?\n\nPatient: Yes, I guess I have. There are mornings when I find myself just standing by the front door with my purse in my hand for a long time. I'm not going out, and... I don't really know what I was about to do. It just doesn't make sense to me when I catch myself doing that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think all these things that are happening to you, like the voices and the messages, are all connected in some way?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure if they're connected. I haven't really thought about it like that. I just know the voices happen, and then sometimes I feel like people are watching me or sending me messages, but... Um... when I was at my absolute lowest—when I was barely eating and just sitting there thinking I had completely failed everyone in my life... that was actually the exact same time I was hearing my friend's voice. And... it was during that time I really believed the people from church were sending those messages about me. So, no, they kind of happened all together, rather than separately.\n\nDoctor: Have you been doing things that you later realize don't make sense, or that other people point out don't make sense?\n\nPatient: Well, like I mentioned a minute ago, it's really just that thing with my purse by the front door. I can't think of anything else besides that. And... no, nobody else has pointed anything out to me. It's mostly just me catching myself doing that one thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you been engaging in any behaviors that you or others consider bizarre or unusual?\n\nPatient: I think that's basically what we were just talking about... um, it's really just the purse thing I mentioned a second ago. Besides that, no. I haven't been doing anything bizarre, and nobody has told me I'm acting unusual.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble completing tasks or activities due to disorganization or difficulty following instructions?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I can still get things done around the house just fine. I haven't noticed any trouble following instructions or anything like that. So, no, I wouldn't say I'm disorganized in that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant changes in your mood or emotional state over the past eleven months?\n\nPatient: Well, yes, I have. Over this past year... um, when I was at my absolute lowest, I was just so down that I was barely eating. I just kept thinking I had completely failed everyone in my life. And... well, what was really hard was that while I was feeling that low, I was also hearing my friend's voice when she wasn't even there. And I really started believing that the people at my church were sending secret messages about me.
